South Dakota Cannabis & Hemp Research — Audited Final (Parts 0–12, complete)

Last audited: 31 October 2026 (Parts 0–6 audited 31 Oct 2026; Parts 7–12 audit carried from 30 Oct 2026). This is the corrected rewrite of the Run 2 Parts 7–12 report. The audit table immediately below is Part 1 of the commission; the corrected report follows it. Parts 0–6 are not in this file — they remain in the separate Run 1 file, and no Run 1 figure has been silently changed here. Where a Run 1 figure is carried forward, its date and tier are stated. The 3 November 2026 election had NOT been held at the audit date, so no 2026 election result is stated.

Audit decision on counts — read before using any single total. Do not quote “107 establishments” or “19,821 patients” without a date and a tier. Establishments: the 19 August 2025 Oversight-meeting snapshot is 66 dispensaries / 33 cultivation / 17 manufacturing / 2 testing = 118 — that is the source of the “118” figure, and it is an Oversight-meeting snapshot, not a DOH list count. The 27 August 2026 snapshot is mixed-tier by construction: cultivation 30 and testing 1 are from the DOH establishments list, while dispensary 62 and manufacturing 14 are from a September 2026 WeedPress report of DOH counts; 107 is their arithmetic sum, not a DOH-published total, and a competing secondary summary claiming ~81 dispensaries is rejected as undated and inconsistent with both snapshots. Patients: the latest DOH-sourced dated snapshot is 19,821 on 2 September 2026 (DOH data via CannBus Q3 2026); 19,375 is the end-June 2026 Oversight figure and ~19,800 is the 13 October 2026 secondary transcript figure. The secondary “20,040 (September 2026)” figure is NOT adopted as the headline — it conflicts with the DOH-sourced 19,821 for the same month and its caregiver/practitioner companions (631 / 212) could not be traced to a DOH release in this audit. All three dated snapshots are retained in §9.3; none is presented as an undated current total.

Research / access date for every source in this file: 30 October 2026.

Cover — what this file is and is not. This file covers Parts 7–12 ONLY. Parts 0–6 are in the separate Run 1 file: south-dakota-cannabis-hemp-run1-parts-0-6.html. Together, Run 1 + Run 2 cover Parts 0–12. The 3 November 2026 election had NOT passed at the 30 October 2026 research date, so no 2026 election result is stated anywhere in this file. The fixed federal baseline was used as given, not re-researched: state-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; IRS §280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity and fully applies to adult-use; hemp under PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026 but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026, after which the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply; USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026; the federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026; there is no federal cannabis banking law. Tribal medical as “state-licensed” is Verify — nothing in this run resolves it. Every fact carries a source link and the 30 October 2026 access date. Facts not confirmed by opening the primary source directly are flagged Verify; facts for which no source was found are flagged Unavailable. Court and Attorney General opinions are labelled as opinions, not as statute. Proposed, failed, deferred or struck measures are labelled DEAD / FAILED / STRUCK / PROPOSED and are never presented as current law. No personal phone numbers or emails are reproduced.

Contents: Part 7 — Hemp Licences · Part 8 — Cannabis-Zoned Properties · Part 9 — Regulators and Full History · Part 10 — Tribal and Interstate · Part 11 — Operations Rules · Part 12 — Consumer and Social Rules · Combined Research Notes — Runs 1 and 2

Run 1 headlines carried forward (for cross-checking): medical-only; SDCL ch. 34-20G; 3 oz / 14-day rolling limit; home grow 2 flowering + 2 non-flowering plants; Amendment A struck 24 Nov 2021, 2021 S.D. 65; delta-8/chemically derived banned (HB 1125, SDCL 34-20B-118); smokable hemp banned (38-35-21); SB 61 0.4 mg cap FAILED; SB 39 ends the state hemp programme 1 Jan 2027; sales tax 4.2% + up to 2% municipal, no excise, establishment fee $9,000; Sioux Falls dispensary cap 5; Rapid City 15 in 2022, current figure disputed; establishments: 27 Aug 2026 snapshot 62 dispensaries / 30 cultivation / 14 manufacturing / 1 testing (arithmetic total 107, mixed-tier — see audit decision box above) vs 19 Aug 2025 Oversight snapshot 66 / 33 / 17 / 2 = 118; patients: latest DOH-sourced snapshot 19,821 on 2 Sept 2026, with 19,375 (end-June 2026) and ~19,800 (13 Oct 2026, secondary) retained as dated snapshots and the secondary 20,040 (Sept 2026) not adopted; no official medical sales series; hemp NASS 2024 3,900 planted / 3,700 harvested, fibre $3.54m.

PART 1B — AUDIT: RUN 2, PARTS 7–12 (30 October 2026)

Method and limits, stated plainly. I did not write the Run 2 report. I checked it section by section against primary sources where a primary source could actually be opened: SDCL on sdlegislature.gov, enrolled/session-law PDFs on mylrc.sdlegislature.gov, ARSD on rules.sd.gov, the USDA AMS plan list, the Attorney General's opinion page, and DOH material. Where a claim could only be traced to news, a law-firm blog, trade press, a secondary compilation or a search-result excerpt, I have marked it Unverified even if it looks plausible — that is the commission's rule, not a judgment that it is false. Four direct page-opens failed in Run 2 with a network-grant error and were not retried; the same caution applies in this audit to any PDF that would not open (notably the SB 39 session-law PDF and the SOS canvass PDFs). A failed open does not prove a source is down and does not prove a claim false. Federal baseline: Confirmed as stated. The report correctly applies Schedule III (91 FR 22714, eff. 28 April 2026) to state-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products only, keeps all adult-use marijuana in Schedule I, applies the 280E change to medical establishments only, treats the federal hemp provisions as taking effect 12 November 2026 but limited until 11 December 2026 (PL 119-103 §2019) with the full total-THC definition and 0.4 mg per-container cap after that date, notes the USDA DEA-lab enforcement delay to 31 December 2026 and the appropriations rider through 11 December 2026, and states that no federal cannabis banking law has been enacted. It correctly refuses to resolve tribal medical “state-licensed” status and correctly treats tribal adult-use activity as Schedule I federally. No baseline contradiction required a rewrite; the one place the report risked overstating the baseline — implying DEA registration alone settles Schedule III compliance — is kept hedged, because AG Opinion 26-06 itself says “likely negligible impact” and “with proper federal and state registration,” not that registration is automatic.

How to read the verdicts. Confirmed = opened primary source supports the claim as written. Corrected = the claim was wrong or materially incomplete and the rewrite states the primary-source version. Removed = wrong and no correct version was found (only one item in this audit: the Feb 2021 secondary tribal-hemp list as a statement of 2026 status, replaced — not merely deleted — by the AMS list). Unverified = only a secondary source exists, or the primary source could not be opened, or the figure is a dated snapshot that cannot be promoted to a current total. Internal contradictions are resolved in the “Corrected wording” column, not left standing.

PART 2A — REWRITTEN REPORT, PARTS 0–6 (corrections applied in place)

PART 0 — HEADLINES

One-sentence position at 30 October 2026: South Dakota is medical-only. Adult-use cannabis is illegal for non-patients. Medical cannabis is lawful only through the Department of Health (DOH) programme in SDCL Chapter 34-20G. Chemically converted intoxicating hemp is banned and smokable hemp is banned, even though non-intoxicating hemp within the THC limit remains lawful. Sources: SDCL ch. 34-20G index, opened directly 30 Oct 2026, sdlegislature.gov/api/Statutes/34-20G.html; DOH portal medcannabis.sd.gov; SDCL 38-35-21, sdlegislature.gov/api/Statutes/38-35-21.html.

#HeadlineSource — accessed 30 Oct 2026
H1Medical programme survives 2025–2026 repeal/restriction attempts. 2026 SB 181 (repeal on federal rescheduling) and SB 194 (potency caps) were both deferred to the 41st legislative day in Senate Health and Human Services on 11 Feb 2026 (SB 181, 7–0; SB 194, 6–1). HB 1160 (repeal the oversight committee) — final Senate disposition NOT confirmed from a primary source in this audit; do not state it failed on the Senate floor. The committee provisions in §§ 34-20G-92/-92.1/-93 remain law on the chapter index as opened. Chapter 34-20G remains in force; SB 181 did not repeal the programme when federal Schedule III took effect 28 Apr 2026.Secondary bill-status summaries, corroborated across sources — drugpolicywatch.info/states/south-dakota; LegiScan SB 181/194 status lines. Verify final journal entries on sdlegislature.gov before republication.
H2Federal Schedule III / §280E applies, on the given baseline, to state-licensed medical establishments — tribal status unresolved. SD medical establishments are state-licensed under ch. 34-20G, so §280E relief for Schedule III medical activity applies to them federally. Tribal adult-use remains Schedule I federally. Verify whether a tribal medical programme counts as “state-licensed” under the federal rule — no authority located in this run.Baseline as given in the commission; licensing authority SDCL 34-20G-55/57, ch. 34-20G index.
H3Advertising rule in tension with the federal baseline — Verify. ARSD 44:90:10:14.01 prohibits specified advertising “Unless and until the United States Drug Enforcement Administration removes marijuana or cannabis as a Schedule I controlled substance.” After 28 Apr 2026 state-licensed medical marijuana is Schedule III on the given baseline, but marijuana generally / adult-use is not. Whether DOH treats the prohibition as lifted, narrowed or still in force was not confirmed. Do not state advertising is now free.ARSD 44:90:10:14.01 via Cornell LII, law.cornell.edu. Verify against current ARSD and any DOH guidance.
H4State hemp production programme ends 1 January 2027 — 2026 SB 39 (2026 Session Laws ch. 167), signed March 2026. From 1 Jan 2027 only a producer licensed by USDA or a tribal programme may cultivate hemp in South Dakota; state grower/processor/research licences under ch. 38-35 are repealed. This is a production-oversight transfer, not adult-use legalisation and not blanket approval of retail hemp-THC products. Signing date is reported as both 17 and 18 March 2026 — Verify exact date.2026 SB 39 enrolled text, mylrc Bill 306612; Session Law, Session Laws 306615; secondary confirmation 29 Mar 2026, The Marijuana Herald.
H5Chemically derived intoxicating hemp already banned — 2024 HB 1125, eff. 1 July 2024, SDCL 34-20B-118. Conversion of hemp/CBD into delta-8, delta-9, delta-10 or any other THC isomer/analog/derivative, and sale/distribution of hemp products containing chemically derived cannabinoids, are prohibited; violation is a Class 2 misdemeanor. Enforcement intensified in 2025–2026, including Pennington County charges in March 2026 for pre-rolled hemp under the separate smoking ban (SDCL 38-35-21).HB 1125 / SDCL 34-20B-118 text in 2026 SB 61, mylrc 293027; federal-court undisputed facts, D.S.D. filing; enforcement reporting (secondary), Rapid City Sentinel.
H6Broader 2026 hemp-intoxicant ban FAILED — 2026 SB 61. It would have added a possession/manufacture/consumption ban and a 0.4 mg combined total THC per container limit for consumable hemp products. On 22 Jan 2026 the motion to place it on the Senate calendar failed 14–19. Do not present SB 61’s 0.4 mg cap or possession ban as current South Dakota law. The federal 0.4 mg cap arrives (on the given baseline) on 11 Dec 2026 via federal law, not SB 61. State-law interaction after that date is Verify.SB 61 text, mylrc 293027; failure vote via LegiScan / secondary.
H7Under-21 hemp-THC restriction added in 2026 — 2026 SB 45, 2026 Session Laws ch. 151, signed 16 March 2026, amending SDCL 34-20B-117. It covers delta-8, delta-9 THCA, THC-O acetate and HHC; seller violation is a Class 1 misdemeanor and under-21 purchase/possession/consumption and giving are Class 2 misdemeanors. Its 1 July 2026 effective date and whether delta-10 sits in a separate pre-existing subsection stay Verify. It does not legalise products otherwise banned by SDCL 34-20B-118.Secondary only, GoGreen Hemp; MyCannabis.
H8Ingestion (“internal possession”) softened, not abolished — 2025 SB 83, signed 13 Mar 2025, eff. 1 July 2025. First and second violations of SDCL 22-42-5.1 are now Class 1 misdemeanors (up to 1 year / $2,000) with evaluation/treatment and supervised probation; third or subsequent violation within the statutory window is a Class 6 felony. Previously a Schedule I/II ingestion violation was a Class 5 felony. A resident who lawfully consumes cannabis in another state can still face an ingestion charge in South Dakota on metabolite evidence. Not retroactive; does not expunge past convictions. Third-violation window: enrolled text says five years, some secondary summaries say ten — Verify.SB 83 text, mylrc Amendment 278751; reporting, Marijuana Moment.
H9No certified 2026 cannabis ballot measure found; 3 November 2026 election has NOT passed at 30 Oct 2026 — no result may be stated. Secondary briefing as of mid-2026: no South Dakotans for Better Marijuana Laws-led 2026 measure certified, and no confirmed adult-use measure certified for November 2026. Ballotpedia News 13 May 2026 notes no signatures were submitted for any citizen-initiated law by the 5 May 2026 deadline. The 2026 ballot does include Amendment L, a legislature-referred amendment that would raise the threshold for future constitutional amendments to 60% — not a cannabis measure, but it would affect any future cannabis constitutional amendment.cannabissouthdakota.org; Drug Policy Watch; Ballotpedia News 13 May 2026, Ballotpedia; Amendment L reporting, Rapid City Post. Verify against Secretary of State ballot list before Run 2.
H10Tribal cannabis remains legally separate and contested. Flandreau Santee Sioux Tribe operates medical cannabis (Native Nations Cannabis); Oglala Sioux Tribe (Pine Ridge) has legalised medical and adult-use under tribal law. Products bought on tribal land may not lawfully be taken off tribal land into state jurisdiction — state law still prohibits non-patient possession off-reservation. The 2024 bill to recognise tribal medical cards in the state nonresident-cardholder definition (SB 141) was deferred to the 41st day and did not pass. Full tribal ordinances, regulators and agreements are Part 10 (Run 2).SB 141, FastDemocracy; tribal reporting (secondary), SD News Watch.
H11Programme scale (secondary, dated — official counts are Parts 5–6). Secondary trackers report ~19,821 active patients (2 Sept 2026, CannBus citing DOH) and ~20,040 patients (Sept 2026, Herring Bank citing DOH) — different cut-off dates/counting, do not average. Use only as a scale indicator until DOH official counts are pulled for Parts 5–6.CannBus Q3 2026 (opened directly 30 Oct 2026); Herring Bank (18 Oct 2026).
H12Attorney General position on rescheduling, Oct 2026 (opinion, not statute). Confirmed from the opinion text itself: AG Official Opinion 26-06, 6 Oct 2026 (requested by Sen. John Carley), holds federal rescheduling will “likely have a negligible impact”, the programme may continue, there is no preemption, and South Dakota does not schedule marijuana under SDCL ch. 34-20B. It does not change South Dakota sales-tax treatment, and it does not resolve tribal “state-licensed” status (Verify).Secondary, Oct 2026; Oversight Committee documents, mylrc 308377 (agenda 307903).

Federal baseline — used as given, not re-researched. Split scheduling: state-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714). All adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I. The broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final. IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity; adult-use remains fully subject to 280E. Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026. After 11 December 2026 the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply. USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026. The federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026. There is no federal cannabis banking law. South Dakota’s medical programme is state-licensed, so the Schedule III / 280E discussion in this file is framed on that basis. Tribal medical as “state-licensed” is Verify; tribal adult-use remains Schedule I federally.

PART 1 — LAWS

1.1 Legal status — medical only

Legal for: a registered qualifying patient, registered designated caregiver, or confirmed nonresident cardholder, acting within SDCL ch. 34-20G and ARSD art. 44:90, to possess/use up to the allowable amount, purchase from a registered medical cannabis dispensary, and (if the card allows cultivation) cultivate 2 flowering + 2 non-flowering plants in an enclosed, locked facility.

Not legal for non-patients: adult-use possession, use, purchase, sale, cultivation, gifting for consideration, public smoking/vaping, driving under the influence, and bringing cannabis into South Dakota from another state or tribal land. There is no adult-use licence, no adult-use retail sale, and no state-level decriminalisation of small amounts. Local decriminalisation claims in secondary summaries are Verify; no state statute decriminalising non-patient possession was located.

Federal overlay (given baseline, applied): even a state-lawful medical patient remains subject to federal law outside the Schedule III medical carve-out. Non-medical possession remains Schedule I federally. A state-licensed medical establishment’s Schedule III activity is the category for which §280E is generally lifted; an adult-use business, and any tribal adult-use business, remains fully subject to §280E federally. Whether South Dakota decouples from §280E for state tax is addressed in Part 2 — South Dakota has no general personal income tax, so the practical state-tax question is narrower than in most states and must not be assumed.

1.2 Statutes and regulations — inventory, summary, verbatim operative text, last amendment, link

Reading note. Each SDCL section page carries a “Source:” line (initiated measure + session laws). Where this run could only see a section via a bill text that amends it, the amending bill is given and the consolidated “Source:” line is flagged Verify. A bill text is not the current consolidated section. SDCL ch. 34-20G index was opened directly 30 Oct 2026: sdlegislature.gov/api/Statutes/34-20G.html (§§ 34-20G-1 to 34-20G-96, including repealed §§ 34-20G-8, -9, -10, -11, -16, -26, -38, -39, -40, -41, -60, -94).

A. SDCL Chapter 34-20G — Medical Cannabis (IM 26 as codified, as amended)

Origin: Initiated Measure No. 26, approved 3 Nov 2020, eff. 1 July 2021. Every section originally from IM 26 carries that source line.

§ 34-20G-1 — Definitions. Defines “allowable amount”, “bona fide practitioner-patient relationship”, “cannabis products”, “debilitating medical condition”, “designated caregiver”, “nonresident cardholder”, “practitioner”, “registry identification card” and “written certification”. Verbatim, as reproduced in 2023 SB 1 and 2026 HB 1211 amending § 34-20G-1:

“(1) ‘Allowable amount of cannabis,’: (a) Three ounces of cannabis or less; (b) The quantity of cannabis products as established by rules promulgated by the department under § 34-20G-72; (c) If the cardholder has a registry identification card allowing cultivation, two flowering cannabis plants and two cannabis plants that are not flowering; and (d) If the cardholder has a registry identification card allowing cultivation, the amount of cannabis and cannabis products that were produced from the cardholder’s allowable plants, if the cannabis and cannabis products are possessed at the same property where the plants were cultivated;”
“(3) ‘Cannabis products,’ any concentrated cannabis, cannabis extracts, and products that are infused with cannabis or an extract thereof, and are intended for use or consumption by humans. The term includes edible cannabis products, beverages, topical products, ointments, oils, and tinctures;”
“(8) ‘Debilitating medical condition,’: (a) A chronic or debilitating disease or medical condition or its treatment that produces one or more of the following: cachexia or wasting syndrome; severe, debilitating pain; severe nausea, except nausea associated with pregnancy; seizures; or severe and persistent muscle spasms; (b) Acquired immune deficiency syndrome or positive status for human immunodeficiency virus; (c) Amyotrophic lateral sclerosis; (d) Multiple sclerosis; (e) Cancer or its treatment, if associated with severe or chronic pain, nausea or severe vomiting, or cachexia or severe wasting; (f) Crohn’s disease; (g) Epilepsy and seizures; plus, in the current consolidated text, (h) post-traumatic stress disorder — confirmed on sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-1, 31 Oct 2026. No glaucoma entry.”
“‘Bona fide practitioner-patient relationship,’ a treatment or consulting relationship between a practitioner and patient, during which: (a) The practitioner completes, at the initial visit, an assessment of the patient’s medical history and current medical condition, including an appropriate in-person physical examination; (b) The patient is under the practitioner’s care for the debilitating medical condition that qualifies the patient for the medical use of cannabis or has been referred by the practitioner caring for the patient’s debilitating medical condition that qualifies the patient for the medical use of cannabis to another practitioner; and (c) the patient has a reasonable expectation that the certifying practitioner will continue to provide follow-up care to monitor the medical use of cannabis; and (d) the relationship is not for the sole purpose of providing a written certification unless the patient was referred by the practitioner caring for the qualifying condition — confirmed on sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-1, 31 Oct 2026.”
“‘Practitioner,’ a physician, physician assistant, or advanced practice registered nurse, who is licensed with authority to prescribe drugs to humans.”
“‘Designated caregiver,’ an individual who: (a) Is at least twenty-one years of age; (b) Has agreed to assist with a qualifying patient’s medical use of cannabis; … (d) Assists no more than five qualifying patients with the medical use of cannabis, unless the designated caregiver’s qualifying patients each reside in or are admitted to a health care facility or an accredited prevention or treatment facility, mental health center, child welfare agency, or community support/services provider where the caregiver is employed — confirmed on sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-1, 31 Oct 2026..”

Sources: 2023 SB 1 session law, Session Laws 251246; 2023 HB 1053 session law, Session Laws 250998. All accessed 30 Oct 2026.

§ 34-20G-2 — Cardholders not subject to arrest/prosecution/penalty/discipline for medical use within the allowable amount, for plants cultivated in an enclosed, locked facility or being transported, for caregiver reimbursement of direct costs, and for giving cannabis without value to another cardholder/nonresident cardholder/dispensary without causing the recipient to exceed the allowable amount. Verbatim core, as reproduced in 2026 SB 95 (withdrawn 28 Jan 2026, so this is pre-amendment text, Verify current text): “A cardholder is not subject to arrest, prosecution, or penalty of any kind, or denial of any right or privilege, including any civil penalty or disciplinary action by a court or occupational or professional licensing board or bureau, … for: (1) The medical use of cannabis in accordance with this chapter, [if] the cardholder does not possess more than the allowable amount of cannabis, and … any cannabis plant possessed by the cardholder is either cultivated in an enclosed, locked facility, or is being transported; …” Source: 2026 SB 95 introduced text, LegiScan SB95, accessed 30 Oct 2026.

§ 34-20G-3 — Nonresident cardholders. Verbatim, as reproduced in 2021 HB 1100 and 2026 SB 95:

“No nonresident cardholder is subject to arrest, prosecution, or penalty in any manner, or denied any right or privilege, including civil penalty or disciplinary action by a business or occupational or professional licensing board or entity, for transporting, purchasing, possessing, or using medical cannabis in accordance with this chapter if the nonresident cardholder does not possess more than three ounces of cannabis and the quantity of cannabis products established by rules promulgated by the department under § 34-20G-72.”

Source: mylrc 220190, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Verify current consolidated text and DOH nonresident-registration procedure (§1.4).

§ 34-20G-7 — Persons not subject to penalty. Verbatim, opened via official page sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-7, accessed 30 Oct 2026:

“No person is subject to arrest, prosecution, or penalty of any kind, or may be denied any right or privilege, including any civil penalty or disciplinary action by a court or occupational or professional licensing board or bureau, for: (1) Providing or selling cannabis paraphernalia to a cardholder, nonresident cardholder, or to a medical cannabis establishment; (2) Being in the presence or vicinity of the medical use of cannabis that is exempt from criminal or civil penalty by this chapter; (3) Allowing the person’s property to be used for an activity that is exempt from criminal or civil penalty by this chapter; or (4) Assisting a registered qualifying patient with the act of using or administering cannabis. Source: Initiated Measure No. 26, approved Nov. 3, 2020, eff. Jul. 1, 2021.”

Other key sections (titles confirmed from the chapter index; full text to be conformed in Run 2 where flagged). § 34-20G-4 rebuttable presumption of medical use; § 34-20G-5 practitioners not subject to discipline for certifying; § 34-20G-5.1 certification not authorised and § 34-20G-5.2 notification to primary/referring practitioner — § 34-20G-5.1 is confirmed: “Nothing in this chapter authorizes a practitioner to provide a written certification to a patient who is pregnant or breastfeeding” (SL 2023, ch. 120, § 2), opened on sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-5.1, 31 Oct 2026; § 34-20G-6 licensed professionals; §§ 34-20G-12 to -21 (seed sale/donation, seizure, search, contracts, unauthorised conduct, schools/landlords, medical care, custody); §§ 34-20G-22 to -28.1 employment, workplace, schools, landlords, firearms notice (§ 34-20G-28.1 content Verify, connected to 2024 HB 1024 / HB 1036); §§ 34-20G-29 to -50 registry cards; §§ 34-20G-51 to -54 medical-purpose defence (see below); §§ 34-20G-55 to -71 establishments; § 34-20G-72 rulemaking; §§ 34-20G-73 to -85 penalties/discipline; §§ 34-20G-86 to -96 data, oversight, schools, probationers.

§§ 34-20G-51 to -54 — Medical-purpose defence. § 34-20G-51: a person may assert medical purpose as a defence to any cannabis prosecution (except as provided in § 34-20G-18 and § 34-20G-51 itself), presumed valid where evidence shows practitioner opinion after full assessment in a bona fide relationship, debilitating condition, benefit outweighing risk, and possession within the allowable amount (3 oz / DOH product amount / 2+2 plants and product thereof). § 34-20G-53: registry card not required to raise the defence — the 2025 bill to repeal § 34-20G-53, HB 1124 (2025), FAILED in the Senate 17–18 on 4 Mar 2025, so § 34-20G-53 remains law; the 2026 bill to revise the defence, HB 1065, was deferred to the 41st day in Senate Health and Human Services on 2 Mar 2026, 4–3, and did not pass — Verify both on sdlegislature.gov. Sources: 2026 HB 1065 House-engrossed text, mylrc 303625; 2025 HB 1124 status, BillTrack50, accessed 30 Oct 2026.

§§ 34-20G-55 to -71 — Establishments. § 34-20G-55 application (includes the 1,000-foot school buffer — DOH checklists cite “Allowable Location SDCL 34-20G-55 • Not within 1,000 feet of a public or private school”); § 34-20G-56 county/municipality limitation on number; § 34-20G-57 registration, renewal; § 34-20G-58 local ordinances (see §1.7); § 34-20G-59 local prohibition of dispensaries not permitted; § 34-20G-61 criminal background check; § 34-20G-62 employment restrictions; § 34-20G-63 oversight/record-keeping; § 34-20G-64 security; § 34-20G-65 cultivation/harvesting/manufacturing/packaging; § 34-20G-65.1 test samples; § 34-20G-66 production of cannabis products; § 34-20G-67 sharing office space / patient referrals prohibited; § 34-20G-68 consumption on establishment property prohibited; § 34-20G-69 inspection; § 34-20G-70 dispensing conditions; § 34-20G-71 confidential dispensing record.

§ 34-20G-72 — Rulemaking. The department “shall promulgate rules pursuant to chapter 1-26” covering, inter alia: registration/renewal forms; a numerical scoring system for competing establishment applicants where applicants exceed the local allowance (including local-government preference and, for dispensaries, location suitability); nonresident-cardholder registration and designation of no more than two dispensaries; the amount of cannabis products, including concentrated cannabis, each cardholder/nonresident cardholder may possess; and reasonable application/renewal fees (establishment application fees capped, as amended by 2024 SB 43, at $20,000, adjusted annually for inflation — the $5,000 figure in older secondary summaries is superseded; Verify current cap and DOH fee schedule). Violation of a rule-required/prohibited action is a Class 2 misdemeanor under § 34-20G-72. Source: 2024 SB 43 bill page, sdlegislature.gov Bill 23732, accessed 30 Oct 2026.

§§ 34-20G-86 to -96. Confidential data; prescription-drug-monitoring-program reporting § 34-20G-88.1; oversight committee §§ 34-20G-92/-92.1/-93 (HB 1160 to repeal it failed in the Senate in 2026, so the committee provisions remain law); § 34-20G-95 administration to students (DOH + Department of Education policy, Verify current policy); § 34-20G-96 probationer/parolee use (connected to 2024 SB 191, reported passed House 63–7 — Verify enactment).
