A related 2024 estimate ($346,860 partial year / $490,770 full year of increased revenue) is also a projection and conflicts in scope with the $846k / $1.2m figures; the two projection sets are not reconciled in retrieved sources — Verify against the 2024 fiscal note.

Cannabis-specific sales-tax revenue — Unavailable in retrieved sources. No Department of Revenue publication retrieved in this research breaks out sales tax collected specifically on medical-cannabis sales, by year or in total, separately from general retail sales tax. Dispensary sales are remitted as general retail sales; DOR does not appear, in retrieved material, to publish a cannabis-only sales-tax line. Therefore: do not state a total “South Dakota medical-cannabis sales-tax revenue” figure. If the final dossier needs one, the follow-up source is a Department of Revenue data request / the Department of Health annual report’s revenue section, not an estimate derived from patient counts or secondary market reports. Medical sales totals: no official medical-sales-by-year total was confirmed in the sources retrieved for Parts 2–4. Secondary market reports for 2026 (CannBus Q3 2026) discuss patient counts and establishment counts, not an official sales total. Sales totals belong to Part 6 and must not be invented here to back into a tax figure.

Reconciliation note (Parts 2 vs 5–6): The SFY 2024 annual report opened directly for Parts 5–6 gives programme revenue $1,915,946 (cardholder fees $1,141,176; establishment fees $774,770) and expenditures $1,193,010 — consistent with the secondary “$1.9 million” above. That opened-directly figure is the one to cite for SFY 2024; the FY22/FY23 figures above remain secondary-only. Source: SFY 2024 Annual Report, mylrc RequiredReport 273781, opened directly 30 Oct 2026.

PART 3 — LOCAL RULES

3.1 Dual licensing — the state registration is necessary but not sufficient

The rule in one sentence. A medical cannabis establishment in South Dakota needs both (a) a state registration certificate from the Department of Health and (b) whatever local licence, permit or registration the city or county where it will operate requires — plus, where the locality has zoning, a sworn statement that it does not violate that zoning. If either layer is missing, it may not operate.

State layer — SDCL 34-20G-55, opened directly. sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-55, accessed 30 Oct 2026, provides in relevant part:

“Not later than ninety days after receiving an application for a medical cannabis establishment, the department shall register the prospective medical cannabis establishment and issue a registration certificate and a random ten-digit alphanumeric identification number if all of the following conditions are satisfied: (1) The prospective medical cannabis establishment has submitted all of the following: (a) The application fee; (b) An application, including: (i) The legal name…; (ii) The physical address… that is not within one thousand feet of a public or private school existing before the date of the medical cannabis establishment application; (iii) The name and date of birth of each principal officer and board member…; (c) Operating procedures consistent with rules…; (d) If the city or county where the proposed medical cannabis establishment would be located has enacted zoning restrictions, a sworn statement certifying that the proposed medical cannabis establishment does not violate the restrictions; (e) If the city or county where the proposed medical cannabis establishment requires a local registration, license, or permit, a copy of the registration, license, or permit; (2) None of the principal officers or board members has served as a principal officer or board member for a medical cannabis establishment that has had its registration certificate revoked; (3) None of the principal officers or board members is under twenty-one years of age; and (4) At least one principal officer is a resident of this state.”

Source line on the page: “Initiated Measure No. 26, approved Nov. 3, 2020, eff. Jul. 1, 2021.” No later amendment is shown for § 34-20G-55 on that page.

Practical consequence — order varies by locality, and that matters. The statute is written as: if the locality requires a local licence, include a copy with the state application. That implies local first in those localities. Several city codes make that explicit: Sioux Falls § 121.003 requires the city licence before seeking state registration under § 34-20G-55; Meade County Ordinance 53 likewise requires a county licence prior to submitting to the Department of Health. The Department of Health’s establishment checklist (2021) required a Local Government Compliance Certification, Form E, signed by the municipality or county, confirming zoning/ordinance compliance. Form E is the bridge document between the two layers. Verify the current Form E / checklist — the 2021 checklist is the version retrieved; the Department’s 2026 rules package may have revised it.

Local layer — SDCL 34-20G-58 as amended in 2024, opened via search excerpt and enrolled bill. Current § 34-20G-58, sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-58 — the sdlegislature.gov page itself returned only a navigation shell when opened in this research, so the verbatim text below is taken from the enrolled 2024 bill and the search excerpt of the same section, not from a full open of the current page. Verify against the current statute page before final publication:

“The governing body of a municipality may enact an ordinance not in conflict with this chapter, regardless of whether it has enacted a zoning ordinance pursuant to title 11, imposing: (1) Restrictions on a medical cannabis establishment to govern the time, place, and manner of operation; (2) A limit on the number of medical cannabis establishments in the municipality; (3) Reasonable setback requirements; (4) Limitations on the proximity of a medical cannabis establishment to: (a) Any sensitive land-use area, including a childcare facility, park, public service facility, recreational facility, religious facility, school, and any location frequented by individuals under the age of twenty-one; or (b) Any other medical cannabis establishment; (5) Requirements for a medical cannabis establishment to obtain a local license, permit, or registration to operate; or (6) Reasonable fees for any local license, permit, or registration. The governing body of a county may enact an ordinance governing all matters set forth in this section. The county ordinance applies throughout its jurisdiction, except within the boundaries of a municipality that has enacted an ordinance in accordance with this section. A county or municipality may impose a civil penalty for the violation of an ordinance enacted in accordance with this section.”

Source: Initiated Measure No. 26, approved Nov. 3, 2020, eff. Jul. 1, 2021; SL 2024, ch. 137, § 2 (2024 SB 219) — “An Act to modify provisions related to the control of counties and municipalities over medical marijuana establishments within their jurisdictions.” Enrolled bill: mylrc Bill 266929, accessed 30 Oct 2026.

What the 2024 act changed — and what it repealed. SB 219 (SL 2024, ch. 137) did four things relevant here: (1) § 34-20G-56 amended — see below. (2) § 34-20G-58 amended — consolidated local powers into the six-item list above, expressly including “reasonable fees” as item (6). (3) § 34-20G-59 amended — see below. (4) § 34-20G-58.1 repealed and § 34-20G-60 repealed. The former freestanding local-licensing-and-fee section is gone. Its content now lives in § 34-20G-58(5)–(6). Opened directly, § 34-20G-60 now reads simply: “34-20G-60. Repealed. Source: Initiated Measure No. 26, approved Nov. 3, 2020, eff. Jul. 1, 2021; SL 2024, ch 137, § 5.” sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-60 — opened directly, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Do not cite § 34-20G-60 as current authority for a local fee. Cite § 34-20G-58(5)–(6). Older county and city ordinances that recite § 34-20G-60 (e.g. Beadle County’s 2021 ordinance, Butte County’s 2024 draft) are reciting the pre-2024 law; their substance survives only insofar as it is also authorised by current § 34-20G-58.

The numerical-limit mechanism — SDCL 34-20G-56, opened directly. sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-56, accessed 30 Oct 2026:

“34-20G-56. County and municipality limitation on number of medical cannabis establishments. If the governing body of a county has enacted a numerical limit on the number of medical cannabis establishments in the county and a greater number of applicants seek registration, the department shall solicit and consider input from the county as to its preference for registration. If the governing body of a municipality has enacted a numerical limit on the number of medical cannabis establishments in the municipality, and a greater number of applicants seek registration, the department shall solicit and consider input from the municipality as to its preference for registration.”

Source: IM 26, eff. 1 July 2021; SL 2024, ch. 137, § 1. Note the wording: the Department must solicit and consider local preference. It is not, on the face of § 34-20G-56, a delegation that the locality itself picks the winner. The picking mechanism when applicants exceed a local limit is in the rules — scoring, then lottery — see Part 4.4. Some cities run their own lottery first (Sioux Falls, Spearfish) and then forward winners to the state; others rely on the state scoring/lottery (Rapid City). Both patterns exist in the record; neither is the single statewide method.

The anti-ban rule for dispensaries — SDCL 34-20G-59, opened directly. sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-59, accessed 30 Oct 2026:

“34-20G-59. Local government prohibition of dispensaries not permitted. No county or municipality may prohibit a dispensary, either expressly or through the enactment of an ordinance that makes the operation of the dispensary impracticable in the county or municipality.”

Source: IM 26, eff. 1 July 2021; SL 2024, ch. 137, § 3. What § 34-20G-59 does and does not do — read it narrowly. It prohibits banning dispensaries. A locality must allow at least one dispensary in a practicable way. Secondary summaries phrase this as “must allow at least one dispensary.” It does not, on its face, prohibit a locality from: capping dispensaries at a low number (1, 2, 3, 5…); excluding other establishment types — Sioux Falls permits only dispensaries and testing facilities and permits no cultivation or manufacturing facility within city limits; Clay County’s 2021 ordinance allows one dispensary and one manufacturing facility but no cultivation or testing licence — those exclusions are not dispensary bans; imposing time/place/manner, setback, zoning and reasonable-fee conditions that make operation more expensive or more restricted, short of impracticability. A locality that set a dispensary cap of zero, or setbacks/fees so restrictive that no dispensary could practicably operate, would be in tension with § 34-20G-59. No locality in Part 3.3 was found to have done so in a currently effective ordinance, but whether a particular set of conditions crosses into “impracticable” is a fact-specific legal question — Verify for any specific site. A 2024 bill that would have allowed local prohibition, 2024 HB 1150 (Rep. Gross), which proposed amending § 34-20G-58 to allow a locality to prohibit an establishment and amending § 34-20G-60 to protect pre-July 2024 establishments, did not become the law described in § 34-20G-58/59 above. The enacted 2024 law is SB 219, not HB 1150. Do not cite HB 1150 as authority for a ban.

How county and municipal rules interact — the hierarchy. (1) Inside a municipality that has enacted its own § 34-20G-58 ordinance: the municipal ordinance governs. The county ordinance does not apply inside that municipality. (2) Outside any such municipality (unincorporated county, plus joint-jurisdiction / extra-territorial areas where the city and county have agreed a joint rule): the county ordinance governs. (3) Joint / extra-territorial areas are a special case: Pierre + Hughes County agreed no establishment in the 3-mile extra-territorial area around Pierre; Huron + Beadle County share a joint-jurisdiction area with a combined licence count; Brookings city + Brookings County have joint-jurisdiction zoning. In those areas, read the joint ordinance, not just the city or county code alone. (4) State floor: every establishment, wherever it sits, must still satisfy the state 1,000-foot school rule in § 34-20G-55(1)(b)(ii) (school existing before the application date) and all ARSD Article 44:90 operating rules. A local ordinance cannot waive the state floor, and a local ordinance “not in conflict with this chapter” cannot contradict chapter 34-20G. (5) Civil penalty: a county or municipality may impose a civil penalty for violation of its § 34-20G-58 ordinance — § 34-20G-58, final paragraph. This is separate from state fines (up to $10,000 per inspection for multiple/serious violations, SDCL 34-20G-72(6)(a)) and from criminal penalties for unlawful sale/transfer.

Licensing methods — lottery, scoring, first-come, and what was NOT found. State scoring + lottery tie-break: SDCL 34-20G-72(2) requires rules for a numerical scoring system for competing applicants where more apply than the local government allows, considering (a) local-government preference, (b) for dispensaries, location suitability and patient accessibility, (c) character/veracity/background/qualifications/experience of principal officers and board members, and (d) business plan, including for cultivation/dispensary adequate supply, safety/security, anti-diversion procedures, and any plan for low-income patients. ARSD 44:90:03:15 (scoring) and 44:90:03:16 (lottery tie-break + waitlist) implement it. See Part 4.4. Local lottery: Sioux Falls (5 dispensary licences, lottery 17 Nov 2021) and Spearfish (1 dispensary licence, random draw Dec 2021) ran city lotteries. These are local lotteries under city ordinances, not the state ARSD lottery, though the state lottery in Rapid City (9 Mar 2022) is the best-documented state example. Auction: No auction system for medical-cannabis licences was found in any South Dakota statute, rule, or city/county ordinance retrieved in this research. The commission’s framing (“auctions or lotteries”) should not be read as stating that South Dakota uses auctions. If a locality has used an auction-like bidding process, it was not in the retrieved record — Verify; do not assert an auction mechanism. Scoring by the locality itself: some cities (e.g. Brookings, Yankton) use council review / manager review and approval or denial, not a lottery, where applications do not exceed a cap. That is a discretionary approval model, not a lottery model.

Zoning — state floor + local overlay. State: 1,000 feet from a public or private school existing before the application date — § 34-20G-55(1)(b)(ii). Local: cities/counties add their own districts (commercial, industrial, central business, etc.), setbacks from childcare, parks, religious facilities, residential districts, other dispensaries, and hours. Those overlays vary by jurisdiction and are listed in Part 3.3. A site that passes the state 1,000-foot school test can still fail a local 1,000-foot church/park/daycare test, a residential setback, or a district restriction.

3.2 No official statewide allow / ban list — finding

Finding: No official statewide list of jurisdictions that allow or ban medical-cannabis establishments was found on the Department of Health, Department of Revenue, or Secretary of State sites in the sources retrieved. What exists instead: the Department of Health establishments list (medcannabis.sd.gov) lists registered establishments by name/type/location. It shows where establishments are, not which jurisdictions allow them, and it does not list jurisdictions with zero establishments or jurisdictions that cap at a number not yet reached. Individual city and county codes, council minutes, and fee schedules — the sources used in Part 3.3. Secondary compilations (e.g. SouthDakotaStateCannabis.org county pages) that purport to summarise each county were used here only as leads to official ordinances; their county summaries are not treated as official and are flagged where they are the sole source. Consequence: Part 3.3 below is a jurisdiction-by-jurisdiction check, not a reproduction of a statewide list. Every “Verify” in that table means: no official city/county text confirming the point was retrieved in this research, not that the jurisdiction has no rule.

3.3 Jurisdictions checked — minimum list, with dates and sources

Same-name warning applied throughout: All places below are the South Dakota places. Sioux Falls, SD is not Sioux City, IA. Aberdeen, SD is not Aberdeen, WA or Scotland. Brookings, SD is not Brookings, OR. Watertown, SD (Codington County) is not Watertown, NY. Huron, SD (Beadle County) is not Huron, OH. Vermillion, SD (Clay County) is not Vermilion, OH. Sturgis, SD is not Sturgis, MI. Box Elder, SD is not Box Elder, UT. Madison, SD, Hot Springs, SD and Lead, SD are not in the minimum list and are not conflated here; Hot Springs, SD code § 116 appears in one search result and is not used as a Sturgis or other-city rule.

3.3.1 Sioux Falls, SD + Minnehaha County

City of Sioux Falls — Chapter 121 (licensing) + Chapter 160 (zoning), Ordinances 105-21 et seq., Sept 2021. Types allowed in city limits: dispensaries and testing facilities ONLY. § 121.002(b)(1), via American Legal Publishing code library: “No medical or other cannabis establishment, other than a medical cannabis dispensary or testing facilities, shall be permitted to operate within the jurisdictional limits of the city.” Cultivation and manufacturing are not permitted in Sioux Falls city limits. Cap: no more than 5 active medical-cannabis dispensaries at any given time, under SDCL 34-20G-56 authority. § 121.002(b)(2): “there shall be no more than five active medical cannabis dispensaries that may be registered by the state of South Dakota to operate within the jurisdictional limits of the city at any given time.” Testing facilities: no numerical limit in the ordinance as reported at final passage (Sept 2021). Licensing method: city lottery for the 5 dispensary licences. City Licensing Office accepted applications 29 Oct – 15 Nov 2021; lottery 10 a.m., 17 Nov 2021, City Hall Commission Room. SDPB, 25 Oct 2021: SDPB lottery announcement — secondary, but reporting a city announcement. The first licence drawn was The Flower Shop, LLC; it opened 2 Sept 2022 at 2211 W. 49th St. as Sioux Falls’ first dispensary and the third state-licensed dispensary statewide. Fees — final-passage figures, Sept 2021, via code + KELO final-vote reporting: Application fee: $25,000 dispensary / $1,500 testing, refundable if the applicant is denied a state registration certificate or city licence. § 121.003(b)(1), Sioux Falls Code § 121.003 — search-result excerpt. Initial licence fee: $50,000 dispensary / $5,000 testing, covering the remainder of the calendar year of issuance + the full subsequent calendar year. Renewal: $25,000 dispensary / $1,500 testing per KELO final-vote summary, 7 Sept 2021. Verify renewal figures against current § 121.003; the code excerpt retrieved confirms application + initial fees, not the renewal line in full. Earlier proposal figures ($100,000 initial / $100,000 biennial renewal, Aug 2021 mayoral proposal) were amended down before final passage and must not be used as current fees. Order: city licence first, then state registration under § 34-20G-55, then city certificate of occupancy after inspection. § 121.003(a). Zoning / operation: 1,000 feet from sensitive-use areas (schools, churches, etc.) and 1,000 feet from any other dispensary/testing facility at final passage; hours 8:00 a.m.–10:00 p.m. for dispensaries, § 121.002(b)(4); no consumption on premises; no sharing space with another establishment or a practitioner. Verify current Chapter 160 setbacks — the 1,000-foot / 500-foot conditional-use variant reported in Aug 2021 proposals (500 feet from non-school sensitive uses with a conditional-use permit) may not reflect the final zoning text. Sources: Sioux Falls Code §§ 121.002, 121.003 via American Legal Publishing (search excerpts); KELO, 7 Sept 2021 final votes; SDPB, 25 Oct 2021 lottery announcement; Sioux Falls Business, 6 Sept 2022 first opening. All accessed 30 Oct 2026.

Minnehaha County (unincorporated county, outside Sioux Falls and other municipalities with their own ordinances). Ordinance MC 60-21. No commercial cultivation or manufacturing in the county, per secondary county summary citing MC 60-21. Verify against MC 60-21 itself; the ordinance text was not opened in full. One dispensary allowed in the county (unincorporated area), per the same secondary summary. Hours reported as 8 a.m.–10 p.m., closed on holidays; no consumption on premises; premises may not be shared with a practitioner. Secondary only — Verify. Interaction: Sioux Falls’ 5-dispensary cap and Minnehaha County’s 1-dispensary rule are separate caps for separate jurisdictions. Do not add them into a single “Minnehaha 6” without stating the city/county split.

3.3.2 Rapid City, SD + Pennington County

City of Rapid City — Chapter 5.74, Ordinances 6497 / 6505 (2021) + zoning Chapter 17.50.105. Cap as enacted in 2021–2022: 1 dispensary per 5,000 population, which equalled 15 dispensaries in 2022. RCMC § 5.74.070, as described in a 2025 Pennington County circuit-court filing summarising the ordinance: “Rapid City ordinance limits the number of dispensaries to ‘one for every 5,000 of population of the city,’ which limited the number to 15 dispensaries in 2022.” AP reporting at passage (Sept 2021, effective 2 Oct 2021) likewise reports “limits the number of dispensaries to 15 within city limits” and “Licenses cost $5,000 and require an annual renewal fee of the same amount.” State lottery: 47 applicants for Rapid City dispensary certificates all received identical scores under ARSD 44:90:03:15; the Department held a lottery drawing 9 Mar 2022 to award the 15 certificates, with the remainder waitlisted. See Puffy’s, 2025 S.D. 10, summarised in Part 4.4. This is the clearest documented state-lottery case. Current cap — CONFLICTING / Verify: later reporting describes a proposal to cut the cap, first reported as from 10 to 5 (MJBizDaily, 14 Nov 2025, citing KOTA), and a Legal & Finance Committee tabling of Ordinance 6700, a proposed Chapter 5.74 update that would “alter where dispensaries may locate, increase licensing fees and change application and transfer rules,” on 12 Nov (year not fully specified in the excerpt; Citizen Portal article is ~351 days old at Oct 2026, i.e. Nov 2025). The committee did not advance Ordinance 6700 at first reading, leaving existing Chapter 5.74 in place at that time. A 2024 moratorium, Resolution 2024-079 (7 Oct 2024), directed staff not to redistribute/reallocate a disputed Puffy’s licence during the moratorium. Therefore: do NOT state a single current Rapid City cap as settled. The 2022 cap was 15 by the population formula; whether the operative cap in late 2026 is 15, 10, or 5, and whether Ordinance 6700 was later adopted, must be verified against the current RCMC Chapter 5.74 and the latest council minutes before any site decision. Fees at passage: $5,000 licence + $5,000 annual renewal (AP, 2021). Verify current fees; Ordinance 6700 would have increased them, but it was tabled, not adopted, at first reading. Other types: Rapid City’s Chapter 5.74 covers medical-cannabis establishments generally; cultivation/manufacturing/testing are not excluded city-wide in the same way as Sioux Falls, but zoning and the population-formula cap history concern dispensaries specifically. Verify current treatment of non-dispensary types in RCMC 5.74 / 17.50.

Pennington County (unincorporated, outside Rapid City, Box Elder, etc.). Marijuana Ordinance, §§ 326–328, via Pennington County Planning & Zoning checklist and secondary county summary. Caps reported: 3 cultivation facility licences and 3 dispensary licences, Ordinance § 328 A-2. Secondary only — Verify against the current Pennington County ordinance; the checklist itself was retrieved as a search excerpt. Fees reported in the county checklist: $1,000 with the licence application + $4,000 after Department of Health approval and before licence issuance (total $5,000 operating fee pattern), plus a $1,100 total filing fee line and a $3,000 conditional-use / development-plan element for dispensaries in different excerpts. The fee breakdown is garbled across excerpts — Verify the current Pennington County fee schedule; do not budget from the $1,100 / $1,000 / $4,000 fragments alone. Additional reported fees: development-plan fee $1,000, development-plan review for changes $250, annual renewal $5,000, transfer $5,000, late renewal $2,500. All secondary — Verify. Cultivation: outdoor cultivation not allowed in Pennington County per secondary summary; cultivation only in areas shown on the approved floor plan, not exceeding CUP square footage; development plan must address power, water, hazardous materials. Verify against §§ 326–328. Application route: Pennington County Planning Department, 130 Kansas City St., Suite 200, Rapid City, SD 57701; applications by email to the Planning Department address shown on the checklist or in person. Do not reproduce staff emails from secondary pages in the final dossier without confirming they are public contact addresses.

3.3.3 Aberdeen, SD + Brown County

City of Aberdeen. Fees — 2021 first-reading figures, NOT confirmed current: At a 2021 first reading, the council preliminarily approved a $15,000 licence fee for medical-cannabis dispensaries and $5,000 for cultivation, testing and manufacturing, plus a $500 non-refundable application fee, with a $5,000 annual renewal for all four licences. Fees other than the $500 application fee were described as refundable if licensing was unsuccessful. Source: Aberdeen News via MMJDaily, 2021, secondary. A second reading + publication + 20-day waiting period were still required at that time. Therefore: Aberdeen’s current fee schedule and any cap are Verify. The $15,000 dispensary figure is widely repeated in 2026 secondary summaries (“e.g. $15,000 in Aberdeen”) but every trace leads back to the 2021 first-reading report. Do not present $15,000 as a confirmed 2026 fee without checking Aberdeen’s current fee resolution / code. Establishments in Aberdeen exist: Department of Health inspection reports show a dispensary, Dakota Herb, in Aberdeen, SD 57401, inspected 9 July 2025. That proves operation, not the current cap or fee. Cap / zoning: Not found in retrieved sources — Verify against Aberdeen’s current medical-cannabis ordinance and zoning code. Brown County (unincorporated): Not found — Verify. No Brown County medical-cannabis ordinance text was retrieved. Do not assume the city rule applies countywide; under § 34-20G-58 the county rule, if any, applies only outside municipalities with their own ordinances.

3.3.4 Brookings, SD (Brookings County seat; city rule checked; county joint-jurisdiction noted)

City of Brookings — Ordinance 21-028, “An Ordinance Establishing Procedures for the Licensing of Medical Cannabis Establishments in the City of Brookings, South Dakota”. Official city licensing page: cityofbrookings-sd.gov/840/Medical-Cannabis-Licensing — states the Council has implemented licensing “in a manner consistent with SDCL Chapter 34-20G and Article 44:90” and links Ordinance 21-028 and the city application. Ordinance text: Ord-21-028 — search excerpt, not opened in full, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Licence required + state certificate required before conducting business. Each city licence terminates 31 December of the year issued; renewal applications filed at least 30 days before expiration. Transfer: an application to transfer a licence to a different location or to another person/entity must include all information required by ARSD 44:90:03:01. (Ord. 21-028, § 7.) Fees — Resolution 21-075, as reported in a 2021 council memo: $5,000 application fee + $5,000 annual establishment licence fee. The ordinance itself (§§ 9–10) sets the application fee by the ordinance and the annual fee by resolution, payable within 30 days of receiving the state certificate, on a calendar-year basis with no proration. Verify current resolution; the $5,000 + $5,000 figures are 2021. Cap: No numerical cap was stated in the Ordinance 21-028 excerpts retrieved. Do not invent a Brookings cap. The city’s published current licensed establishments, on its licensing page as retrieved, are: Dispensary: The Flower Shop, 1010 6th Street, Brookings, SD; Dispensary: Genesis Farms, 3025 Orchard Drive, Brookings, SD. A 2025 council action (Resolution 25-078, 23 Sept 2025) authorised licensing The Flower Shop LLC as a dispensary, subject to inspection and issuance of state/local compliance certifications. That is consistent with the city page, but the city page is not a guarantee that no other Brookings licence exists — Verify against the Department of Health establishments list for a complete count. Prohibited locations — Ordinance 22-026 (2022), amending Ord. 21-028 § 16(F): licensed premises shall not be in a dwelling unit or a building containing a paediatrician’s office, hotel/motel or lodging facility; a mixed business/residential structure is allowed only if the premises is not in a dwelling unit. First reading 12 July 2022, second reading 26 July 2022, published 29 July 2022. Brookings County / joint jurisdiction: a Sept 2025 joint city/county agenda concerns rezoning in the joint-jurisdiction area; it is not a medical-cannabis cap. Brookings County’s standalone medical-cannabis ordinance, if any, was not retrieved — Verify.

3.3.5 Watertown, SD + Codington County

City of Watertown — Ordinance 21-34 (Title 25) + Ordinance 21-36 (zoning), Sept 2021. Emergency Ordinance 21-22 (June 2021) first allowed the city to deny applications until Department of Health rules were promulgated. It was temporary. Title 25, as proposed / second-reading material, 7 Sept 2021 joint meeting: Watertown CivicWeb 30127 — Limit of 3 medical dispensaries (proposed Title 25 summary; the Plan Commission on 19 Aug 2021 voted 5–1 to recommend approval with an amendment to strike § 25.07, Limitation on Number of Medical Cannabis Dispensaries — so whether the enacted Title 25 retains the 3-dispensary cap is Verify against the enacted Title 25, not the proposal summary). Zoning in the proposal: Dispensary in C-1, C-2, C-3; setbacks 1,000 feet — schools; 500 feet — daycares; 300 feet — public park, pool, recreation facility, library; 300 feet — religious institutions; 500 feet — residential district. (Proposal chart; enacted text Verify.) Current cap, zoning and fees for Watertown in late 2026: Verify against the current Revised Ordinances Title 25 / Title 21 and the current fee resolution. Codington County. 3.3.6 Mitchell, SD + Davison County

City of Mitchell. Cap reported: 5 dispensary licences maximum; no cap for cultivation and manufacturing licences. Source: Mitchell Republic reporting (secondary, via a republished excerpt), describing the city ordinance and noting two dispensaries approved, three tabled on variance issues at that time, including Native Nations Cannabis’ proposed dispensary at 1620 S. Burr St. Verify against Mitchell’s current ordinance; the cap figure is secondary and dated to the early-programme period. Odour rule reported: a cannabis establishment shall not allow emission of gas, vapours, odours, smoke, dust, heat or glare noticeable at or beyond the property line. Same secondary source. Verify against the current code. Hours: Mitchell City Council, 6 Jan 2025, adopted an ordinance extending medical-cannabis dispensary hours (Citizen Portal summary). Prior hours were described as “similar to pharmacies” in a Dec 2024 council summary, with a request to consider a 10 p.m. closing to match neighbouring cities. Current hours: Verify — the Jan 2025 ordinance text was not retrieved. Renewals: Dec 2024 council approved renewals of 2025 liquor and medical-cannabis dispensary licences including Genesis Farms LLC, The Flower Shop LLC and Raftus Enterprise Mitchell, with no substantial changes reported. That confirms operation, not the current cap or fee. Fees: Not found in retrieved sources — Verify. Davison County (unincorporated): Not found — Verify. No Davison County medical-cannabis ordinance text was retrieved.

3.3.7 Yankton, SD (Yankton County seat; city rule checked)

City of Yankton — Ordinance 1052 + Code Chapter 13, §§ 13-140 et seq. Official city packet: “Medical Cannabis Dispensary License Submittal Requirements and Processing Information,” City of Yankton packet — search excerpt, not opened in full, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Cap: 2 medical-cannabis dispensary registrations available for the City of Yankton at the state-review step: “the State awards the applicant one of the two Medical Cannabis Dispensary registrations available for the City of Yankton.” (Packet, Step 9, citing SDCL 34-20G-55.) Process: (1) research / retrieve packet (Ordinance 1052 + state law); (2–4) preliminary plan review for business plan, site plan, security compliance with §§ 13-145 etc.; (5–6) City Manager decision within 30 days of a fully complete application; (7–9) complete state forms, state review; (10) certificate of occupancy before opening. Operations information required: business plan with intended hours and rules demonstrating compliance with §§ 13-140 and 13-151; security/safety description demonstrating compliance with § 13-145. Fees, zoning setbacks, and whether the 2-dispensary figure is a city cap or a state-allocation figure at the time of the packet: Verify against current Yankton Code Chapter 13 and the current packet. The packet is dated to the early programme (2021) and may not reflect later amendments. Yankton County (unincorporated): Not found — Verify.

3.3.8 Pierre, SD + Hughes County — plus Fort Pierre, SD as a separate city, not to be conflated

City of Pierre — Ordinances 1836 (zoning) + 1837 (licensing), adopted 31 Aug 2021. City announcement, Aug 2021: Pierre CivicAlerts 796 and commission minutes, 31 Aug 2021: Pierre Minutes 08312021 — search excerpts, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Caps at adoption: 3 dispensaries in city limits; 1 each for cultivation, manufacturing and testing facilities. (Ordinance 1837 summary in Cannabis Law Report, Sept 2021, and city announcement.) Fee at adoption: $5,000 application fee per facility type, with $2,500 reimbursed if the applicant fails to obtain the state registration certificate. Code text, Chapter 10, Article 16, § 10-16-203: “Application fee of $5,000. The City will reimburse $2,500 for applicants who fail to obtain a registration certificate from the South Dakota Department of Health.” Source: Pierre Article 16 — search excerpt. Zoning at adoption: All four types may locate in the city industrial park, light-industrial or heavy-industrial districts; dispensaries and testing facilities may also locate in central and local business districts. No establishment within 1,000 feet of a public or private school accredited by the South Dakota Department of Education, Ordinance 1836. Hours reported in commission minutes as 8 a.m.–9 p.m. daily; licence must be active at least 90 days in the preceding 12 months (minutes summary). Verify current hours and “active” rule against current Chapter 10, Article 16. Eligibility at adoption: 21+, no felony record in the summary (minutes) / no violent felony within 10 years in the Law Report summary — the two summaries differ; Verify against the current ordinance text. Also: state licence in good standing, good standing with the city, proper zoning, not overdue on city taxes/utilities/fees (Law Report summary). Extra-territorial area (Pierre + Hughes County joint area, up to 3 miles around the city): NO medical-cannabis establishment allowed in that shared area, by joint city/county determination. City announcement, Aug 2021. That is a joint-jurisdiction exclusion, not a city-wide ban, and it must be read with the city’s inside-limits caps above. Current caps/fees in late 2026: Verify against the current Pierre code; the figures above are adoption figures (2021). Hughes County (outside Pierre city limits and outside the extra-territorial area): Not found as a standalone ordinance — Verify. The joint extra-territorial exclusion is the only Hughes County element retrieved.

Fort Pierre, SD — SEPARATE city, Stanley County, across the Missouri River from Pierre — do not merge with Pierre. Fort Pierre Code § 113.06, via American Legal Publishing: application fee $5,000, with $2,500 reimbursed for applicants who fail to obtain a state registration certificate; application must show a physical address meeting Chapter 159 zoning and SDCL chapter 34-20G / ARSD requirements, plus a copy of the state-issued licence. Source: Fort Pierre Code § 113.06 — search excerpt; the code section is Fort Pierre’s, not Pierre’s. Fort Pierre cap / zoning: Verify — not retrieved beyond § 113.06.

3.3.10 Vermillion, SD + Clay County

City of Vermillion — Ordinance 1435 (7 June 2021), Code Chapter 123, as amended by Ordinance 1451 (20 Dec 2021). Code via American Legal Publishing: Vermillion Code Ch. 123 — search excerpt, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Licence required: § 123.05: “No person or business entity shall operate a medical cannabis establishment within the jurisdictional limits of the city unless that person or business entity has first obtained a medical cannabis establishment license from the city.” Term / transfer: Each licence is effective on issuance and terminates 31 December of the year issued. Licences “shall not be transferable to any other person, business entity, or location and shall lapse automatically upon a change of ownership or location. A license issued under this chapter is not a property right.” (Chapter 123, §§ 123.06–123.07 in the excerpt.) Requirements: At least one owner / LLC member must be a South Dakota resident; no sharing office space with / referral to a practitioner (SDCL 34-20G-67); no consumption on the licensed premises (SDCL 34-20G-68); premises only in city zones addressed by Chapter 158; no less than 1,000 feet from the nearest property line of any public or private school, measured from closest property-line points. §§ 123.08–123.09. Licence is in addition to state registration, zoning permits, plat approvals and building/construction permits. Fees: Vermillion City Council in 2021 agreed a fee structure “based on the population of the city and… on a per capita basis,” under an ordinance passed in May 2021 (Ord. 1435) allowing the city to set licensing/application fees. Source: MMJDaily summary of Yankton Daily Press reporting, 2021, secondary. Exact Vermillion fee amounts were NOT retrieved — Verify against Vermillion’s current fee resolution / Chapter 123 fee section. Cap: Not stated in the Chapter 123 excerpts retrieved — Verify. Do not assume a cap from the Clay County figure below. Clay County (unincorporated, outside Vermillion). Temporary Ordinance 2021-02 (June 2021) paused local permits until Department of Health rules were promulgated. Source: Clay County Ord. 2021-02. Permanent Ordinance 2021-05, second reading 14 Sept 2021, enacted: allows one medical-cannabis dispensary and one manufacturing facility; does not allow cannabis cultivation licences or cannabis testing licences. Source: University of South Dakota Volante summary of the county action, secondary. 3.3.12 Box Elder, SD (Pennington + Meade Counties)

City of Box Elder — Master Fee Schedule, Ordinance 775 (2026 fee schedule). Medical-cannabis fees, Division 8-V-1: Dispensary Establishment Licence: $5,000; Cultivation Establishment Licence: $25,000; Testing Establishment Licence: $25,000; Product Manufacturing Establishment Licence: $25,000; Annual renewal, any type: $5,000; Application, any type: $5,000. Reading note: The schedule lists both a type-specific “licence” fee and a separate “application” fee and “annual renewal” fee. Whether the $25,000 cultivation/testing/manufacturing figure is a one-time licence fee in addition to the $5,000 application fee, or an alternative statement of the same fee, is not clear from the excerpt — Verify against the full Ordinance 775 fee table and the Box Elder medical-cannabis code (Division 8-V) before budgeting. The $5,000 dispensary licence figure, by contrast, aligns with the pattern in Pierre, Brookings and Rapid City. Violation fine in the same schedule: operating without a valid licence or in violation of city cannabis ordinances is subject to a maximum $500 per day fine, excluding civil/criminal penalties. Cap, zoning districts, setbacks, hours for Box Elder: Not retrieved — Verify against Box Elder’s medical-cannabis ordinance (Division 8-V) and zoning code. Box Elder straddles Pennington and Meade Counties; the city rule, where it exists, governs inside city limits, and the county rules govern outside them.

3.4 Business concentration — ONLY what official sources say

This section does not rank, estimate or map clusters. It reports only location information that an official source itself states. No official source retrieved in this research states a ranking of cities/counties by establishment concentration, a “cluster” analysis, or a statement such as “most dispensaries are in X.” Any such ranking in a final dossier would be analyst inference and must not be presented as an official finding. The official location source is the Department of Health establishments list (medcannabis.sd.gov). Secondary summaries of that list report, at different dates, different totals, which illustrates why a single “current count” must not be frozen from a secondary page: July 2024 secondary reporting citing the Department of Health website: 78 dispensaries, 21 manufacturing sites, 40 cultivation sites, 3 testing facilities; 13,257 patient cards and 286 practitioners at 19 July 2024. Source: Marijuana Moment / South Dakota Searchlight, Aug 2024, secondary. Mid-2026 secondary summary (CannBus Q3 2026): ~81 dispensaries statewide, 30 licensed cultivation sites at 27 Aug 2026 (citing the Department of Health establishments list). The cultivation figure (30) and the July 2024 figure (40) are not directly comparable without knowing whether they count sites, certificates, or active vs. registered establishments — Verify against the current establishments list before using either. June 2022 Plainsman overview: 67 dispensaries approved statewide, 25 growers in the state, 1 testing facility (Flandreau) at that date. Historic only. City-published location facts that ARE official (city pages, not directories): Brookings city licensing page lists 2 dispensaries by name/address (see §3.3.4). That is a city-published list for Brookings, not a statewide ranking. Huron / Beadle reporting (2022) names 3 Huron dispensaries by address (see §3.3.11). That is contemporary reporting, not a current official list. Directory / commercial listings (Weedmaps, Genesis Farms’ own location list, etc.) were NOT used to establish concentration. A commercial directory showing Genesis Farms locations in Pierre, Yankton, Mitchell, Spearfish and elsewhere proves marketing presence, not licence counts, and must not be converted into a concentration claim. What can be said safely: Establishments are licensed jurisdiction by jurisdiction, so their locations necessarily follow (a) where a city/county has enacted a licensing ordinance and (b) where its cap has not been reached. Sioux Falls (cap 5, cultivation/manufacturing excluded), Rapid City (population-formula cap, disputed current figure), and the county rules above are the binding constraints on where new establishments can locate. The Department of Health list is the follow-up source for an up-to-date, address-level picture; it is inventoried in Part 5, not reproduced here.

PART 4 — LICENCE TYPES

4.1 The only establishment types in current law — four, and no others

SDCL 34-20G-1(17), opened directly at sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-1, accessed 30 Oct 2026:

“‘Medical cannabis establishment,’ a cultivation facility, a cannabis testing facility, a cannabis product manufacturing facility, or a dispensary”

There is no fifth medical-establishment type in current chapter 34-20G — no separate transporter, distributor, microbusiness, delivery-only, or consumption-lounge registration certificate. Transport, delivery and supply are activities within the four types’ definitions, not separate licences. Patient home cultivation is a cardholder authorisation, not an establishment licence.

Definitions — verbatim from SDCL 34-20G-1, opened directly:

TypeVerbatim definition (SDCL 34-20G-1)Who it may sell / supply to
Cultivation facility — § 34-20G-1(7)“an entity registered with the department pursuant to this chapter that acquires, possesses, cultivates, delivers, transfers, transports, supplies, or sells cannabis and related supplies to a medical cannabis establishment”Only to a medical-cannabis establishment (not directly to patients)
Cannabis product manufacturing facility — § 34-20G-1(4)“an entity registered with the department pursuant to this chapter that acquires, possesses, manufactures, delivers, transfers, transports, supplies, or sells cannabis products to a medical cannabis dispensary”Only to a dispensary (on the face of the definition)
Cannabis testing facility / testing facility — § 34-20G-1(5)“an independent entity registered with the department pursuant to this chapter to analyze the safety and potency of cannabis”Testing service; it does not sell cannabis to patients. “Independent” is in the definition — see co-ownership Verify note below
Medical-cannabis dispensary — § 34-20G-1(16)“an entity registered with the department pursuant to this chapter that acquires, possesses, stores, delivers, transfers, transports, sells, supplies, or dispenses cannabis, cannabis products, paraphernalia, or related supplies and educational materials to cardholders”Only to cardholders (qualifying patients / designated caregivers, including registered nonresident cardholders as provided in chapter 34-20G)

Related definitions, same section, opened directly: “Cannabis products,” § 34-20G-1(3): “any concentrated cannabis, cannabis extracts, and products that are infused with cannabis or an extract thereof, and are intended for use or consumption by humans. The term includes edible cannabis products, beverages, topical products, ointments, oils, and tinctures.” “Medical cannabis establishment agent,” § 34-20G-1(18): “an owner, officer, board member, employee, or volunteer at a medical cannabis establishment.” Source line for § 34-20G-1: IM 26, eff. 1 July 2021; SL 2022, chs. 114–120; SL 2023, chs. 120–122; SL 2024, ch. 132, § 1.

4.2 Type-by-type table — what each allows, caps, fees, requirements

Common to all four types — read this once, it applies to every row below.

ItemRule
State instrumentRegistration certificate + random 10-digit alphanumeric identification number, SDCL 34-20G-55. Not called a “licence” at state level; cities/counties call their instrument a licence/permit/registration. Do not conflate the two in forms.
State capNone. No statewide numerical cap on any of the four types in SDCL chapter 34-20G or in the ARSD excerpts retrieved. Caps are local only, under SDCL 34-20G-56/58.
Local approvalRequired if the locality requires it — SDCL 34-20G-55(1)(d)–(e) + 34-20G-58(5). Where required, the local licence/permit (and, where applicable, Form E compliance certification) must be in hand for the state application. Some localities require local first; see Part 3.1.
State fee, initial + renewal$9,000, non-refundable, per certificate — see Part 2.4. One fee per certificate; a business holding cultivation + manufacturing + dispensary certificates pays per certificate, not once for the business. Verify current ARSD 44:90:03:17 in the 2026 rules package.
Statutory fee ceiling$20,000, inflation-adjusted, SDCL 34-20G-72(10), opened directly. The $9,000 fee is below the ceiling; a future rulemaking could raise it up to the ceiling.
Decision deadline (state)Department shall register not later than 90 days after receiving an application, if all § 34-20G-55 conditions are satisfied.
Renewal deadline (state)Department shall issue a renewal certificate within 45 days of receipt of the prescribed renewal application + renewal fee — SDCL 34-20G-57, opened directly at sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-57, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Source: IM 26; SL 2024, ch. 132, § 3 (which changed the period from the earlier 10-day formulation). Renewal is annual — ARSD 44:90:03:02 requires a renewal application every 12 months from issuance, per the Puffy’s summary.
Principal officers / boardAll 21+; at least one principal officer must be a South Dakota resident; none may have served as a principal officer/board member for an establishment whose certificate was revoked; criminal background check required (SDCL 34-20G-61); disqualifying felony = a violent crime classified as a felony where convicted (SDCL 34-20G-1(11)).
LocationPhysical address not within 1,000 feet of a public or private school existing before the application date (state floor), plus local zoning/setbacks.
Operating proceduresMust submit procedures consistent with ARSD oversight rules, including accurate record-keeping and adequate security — SDCL 34-20G-55(1)(c). Type-specific procedure rules: ARSD 44:90:03:05 (all establishments), :06 (cultivation), :07 (testing), :08 (manufacturing), :09 (dispensing) — as cited in the 2021 Department of Health checklist. Verify current ARSD numbering after the 2026 amendments.
Sales-tax IDDepartment of Health checklist (2021) requires a South Dakota sales-tax ID; applications subject to the 1 Nov 2021 deadline could supplement it within 14 days. Verify current checklist requirement.
Multiple types / co-locationAn applicant may apply for multiple establishment types, but individual certificates are required for cultivation, product manufacturing and dispensary operations (2021 checklist). Whether one legal entity may hold all types at one site depends on the locality (e.g. Lincoln County’s county licence, in secondary summary, allows one cultivation + one manufacturing + one testing + one dispensary at the same site; Sioux Falls forbids cultivation/manufacturing in the city at all). Verify co-location / common-ownership rules for testing facilities specifically — the definition calls testing facilities “independent,” and one secondary summary states cultivators may obtain other licences “except for testing facility licences.” That restriction was not confirmed in the statute excerpts opened and is flagged Verify.
Agent badgesHistoric fee $50 per agent badge (2021). Agents are owners, officers, board members, employees and volunteers. Background checks apply to agents/employees/volunteers under SDCL 34-20G-61. Verify current badge fee and process.

Cultivation facility. Allows: acquire, possess, cultivate, deliver, transfer, transport, supply or sell cannabis + related supplies to a medical-cannabis establishment (not to patients). Includes indoor and outdoor cultivation standards under SDCL 34-20G-72(4)(e) and ARSD rules, subject to local bans on outdoor cultivation (e.g. Pennington County: outdoor not allowed — secondary). State cap: None. Local cap: as in Part 3.3 (e.g. Pennington County 3 reported; Pierre 1; Spearfish 2 reported; Sioux Falls 0 — not allowed in city). Fees: state $9,000 + local fee (varies: e.g. Box Elder $25,000 licence figure in 2026 schedule — reading Verify; Pierre $5,000; Brookings $5,000 + $5,000 in 2021). Special requirements: cultivation operating procedures (ARSD 44:90:03:06 in 2021 numbering); security, record-keeping, pesticide restrictions injurious to health (SDCL 34-20G-72(4)(d)); seed sale/donation rules (SDCL 34-20G-12). Plant-count limits for a cultivation facility are NOT stated as a single statewide number in the excerpts retrieved — Verify ARSD cultivation rules; do not apply the patient home-grow limit (2 + 2) to a facility.

Cannabis product manufacturing facility. Allows: acquire, possess, manufacture, deliver, transfer, transport, supply or sell cannabis products to a dispensary. This is the only establishment type that may perform solvent extraction of resin in the course of manufacturing (patient “medical use” definition excludes solvent extraction unless done by a manufacturing facility, SDCL 34-20G-1(19)(c)). State cap: None. Local cap: as in Part 3.3 (e.g. Pierre 1; Spearfish 2 reported; Sioux Falls 0; Clay County 1). Fees: state $9,000 + local fee (same local variation as cultivation). Special requirements: manufacturing operating procedures (ARSD 44:90:03:08 in 2021 numbering); standards for safe manufacture of extracts/concentrates (SDCL 34-20G-72(4)(h)); food-service licence reported as a prerequisite for manufacturing in August 2026 secondary reporting (CannaCon) — Verify; the food-service requirement was not confirmed in statute/ARSD excerpts opened. Note on edibles/beverages/tinctures: all are “cannabis products” / “edible cannabis products” under § 34-20G-1(3) and (12), including beverages, oils and tinctures. Labelling, including onset time, ingredients/allergens, nutrition panel and a standard cannabis symbol for edibles where practicable, is required by SDCL 34-20G-72(7). Detailed labelling/packaging rules are Part 11 material.

Dispensary. Allows: acquire, possess, store, deliver, transfer, transport, sell, supply or dispense cannabis, cannabis products, paraphernalia, related supplies and educational materials to cardholders only. May not dispense more than 3 ounces of cannabis to a patient / nonresident cardholder, directly or via a caregiver, in any 14-day period (SDCL 34-20G-71, as summarised in secondary sources; the 3-ounce / 14-day limit is also the “allowable amount” in § 34-20G-1(1)(a)). State cap: None. Local cap: the most commonly capped type — see Part 3.3 for every required jurisdiction (Sioux Falls 5, Rapid City population formula / disputed, Yankton 2, Pierre 3, Spearfish 1, Watertown 3 proposed, Mitchell 5 reported, etc.). A locality may cap dispensaries but may not prohibit them — SDCL 34-20G-59. Fees: state $9,000 + local fee, which is highest for dispensaries in several cities (Sioux Falls $25,000 application + $50,000 initial; Aberdeen $15,000 in 2021 first reading; Box Elder $5,000; Watertown $25,000 application in 2025 resolution — reading Verify; Sturgis $7,500 renewal in 2024 agenda). Special requirements: dispensing procedures (ARSD 44:90:03:09 in 2021 numbering); verify cardholder identity + valid card + 14-day limit before dispensing (SDCL 34-20G-70/71 framework); no sharing office space with / referral to a practitioner (SDCL 34-20G-67); no consumption on dispensary property (SDCL 34-20G-68); hours and setbacks are local (e.g. Sioux Falls 8 a.m.–10 p.m.). Designation: nonresident cardholders designate no more than two dispensaries under SDCL 34-20G-72(8) rules. Resident patients are not limited to a single designated dispensary in the statute excerpts retrieved — Verify the current designation rule in ARSD; older patient guides that told patients to “skip dispensary designation unless aware of an approved dispensary” are 2021 material.

Testing facility. Allows: analyse the safety and potency of cannabis as an independent entity. It does not dispense to patients and, on the face of the definition, does not sell cannabis products to dispensaries. State cap: None. Local cap: usually uncapped where allowed (Sioux Falls: testing facilities not limited by city ordinance at final passage; Spearfish 2 reported; Pierre 1; Clay County 0 — no testing licence allowed). Fees: state $9,000 + local fee (Sioux Falls $1,500 application + $5,000 initial + $1,500 renewal at final passage; Box Elder $25,000 licence figure — reading Verify; other cities $5,000 pattern). Special requirements: testing operating procedures (ARSD 44:90:03:07 in 2021 numbering); certification standards for testing facilities, including equipment and personnel qualifications (SDCL 34-20G-72(4)(k)); sampling rules, including batch sizes not to exceed 50 pounds of cannabis intended for retail sale and representative sampling (SDCL 34-20G-72(4)(l)). The identity / count of currently registered testing facilities is Part 5 / Part 11 material; July 2024 secondary reporting put the statewide total at 3, and a 2026 secondary summary names one lab in Flandreau — both are secondary and the current count is Verify against the Department of Health establishments list. Independence Verify: whether a testing facility may share ownership, premises or staff with a cultivation/manufacturing/dispensary operator, beyond the word “independent” in the definition, was not confirmed in opened statute text. Do not advise on testing-facility ownership structure without checking ARSD 44:90 testing rules and any colocation rule (ARSD 44:90:04:04, colocation, cited in a 2025 Department of Health inspection report for a dispensary).

4.3 Ownership, transfer, renewal, suspension — state rules

Transfer of ownership — ARSD 44:90:03:04, via search excerpt of the current rules compilation:

“The transfer of any ownership interest in a medical cannabis establishment of 50 percent or more to a party not already approved by the department as an owner of the establishment requires the submission of an initial application pursuant to § 44:90:03:01. The department may permit the transfer of an ownership interest in a medical cannabis establishment of less than 50 percent to a party not already approved by the department as an owner of the establishment if: [conditions including] The establishment is operating in substantial compliance with its department-approved operating procedures or can demonstrate that circumstances beyond its control prevented such operation; The establishment provides advance written notification to the department; The new owners meet all requirements of this article; and (4) The city or county issues any required registration, license, or permit to the establishment’s new owners. Nothing in this section shall be interpreted to require a city or county to approve a transfer of ownership.”

Source: ARSD 44:90:03:04, 48 SDR 40, eff. 5 Oct 2021; General Authority SDCL 34-20G-72(2); Law Implemented SDCL 34-20G-55(1), 34-20G-57, 34-20G-61 — via rules compilation search excerpt, sdlegislature.gov Rules 40176 and the Jan 2026 ARSD Article 44:90 compilation, accessed 30 Oct 2026. Verify against the current ARSD text; the 2026 rules package may have amended § 44:90:03:04.

Practical points. ≥50% to a new (unapproved) owner = new initial application, with a new $9,000 fee on the current fee structure and full § 34-20G-55 vetting. It is not a mere notice filing. <50% to a new owner = Department may permit it, on the four conditions above, including local issuance to the new owners. The locality is not required to approve the transfer — a local refusal can therefore block a state-permitted minority transfer in practice. Local transfer rules are stricter in some cities: Vermillion licences are non-transferable and lapse automatically on ownership or location change. Sioux Falls licences are transferable only under § 121.003 criteria (final-passage summary). Brookings requires a transfer application with full ARSD 44:90:03:01 information. Always check the city/county transfer rule in addition to ARSD 44:90:03:04. Historic state transfer fee $250 (2021) — Verify current fee; it may have been superseded by the $9,000 initial-application requirement for ≥50% transfers.

Location transfer — ARSD 44:90:03:03. A location-transfer application rule exists (ARSD 44:90:03:03, “Certificate location transfer — Application,” listed in the ARSD chapter table). Its substantive conditions and fee were not retrieved in full — Verify. Historic fee $250 to operate at a different physical location (2021) is not confirmed current. A location move also triggers a new local zoning / licence analysis and the state 1,000-foot school test at the new address.

Renewal. State: annual, every 12 months from issuance (ARSD 44:90:03:02); Department issues within 45 days of renewal application + fee (SDCL 34-20G-57). Renewal fee = initial fee ($9,000 current). Non-renewal notice for lottery-issued certificates: ARSD 44:90:03:02.01 (2023 revision) requires an establishment that does not intend to renew to notify the Department at least 45 days before expiration; if it was lottery-issued, the next lottery waitlist applicant may apply. Verify current § 44:90:03:02.01 after the 2026 amendments. Local renewal: calendar-year in many cities (Brookings, Vermillion: terminate 31 Dec; renewal 30 days before), 45–90 days before expiration in Sturgis, annual in Rapid City / Pierre patterns. Local renewal is separate from state renewal; missing either can close the business. One-year operational rule (litigated): under ARSD 44:90:03:16 as described in Puffy’s, a successful registration-certificate recipient has one year to become operational “or the certificate is deemed void and must be awarded to the next applicant on the waiting list.” The Supreme Court in Puffy’s held that a waitlist applicant is neither an initial nor a renewal applicant for fee purposes under the rules as then written, and addressed the validity / application of the one-year rule. Do not rely on the one-year rule’s current form without checking the post-Puffy’s ARSD text and any Department guidance — the 2026 rules package may have revised it.

Suspension / revocation / fines — state. SDCL 34-20G-80/81 (suspension/revocation, notice/hearing/duration) and SDCL 34-20G-72(6) (fines up to $10,000 per inspection for multiple/serious violations; probation/suspension/termination procedures) are the state enforcement framework. Detailed grounds and timelines are Part 11 material and were not opened in full for this Part 4 report. Verify §§ 34-20G-80/81 text before advising on enforcement risk. A revoked establishment’s former principal officers/board members are barred from new applications under § 34-20G-55(2).

Ownership caps. No statewide ownership cap (e.g. no “one entity may hold no more than X dispensaries statewide”) was found in SDCL chapter 34-20G or ARSD excerpts retrieved. Caps operate per locality, not per owner statewide. Per-establishment ownership conditions (21+, SD-resident principal officer, background check, no revoked-establishment history) apply to every certificate an owner holds. Vertical integration: no statute retrieved prohibits one entity from holding cultivation + manufacturing + dispensary certificates (subject to local type exclusions and the testing-independence question). Several operators in fact hold multiple types. Verify any ARSD vertical-integration or colocation restriction before assuming a multi-type site is permissible in a given city.

4.4 Whether registrations are open, and how oversubscribed localities are decided

Answer: open in principle, closed in practice where a local cap is full — and the decision method is scoring, then lottery. Department of Health programme page still publishes establishment tools: South Dakota Department of Health, Medical Cannabis programme, doh.sd.gov/programs/medical-cannabis-program and medcannabis.sd.gov — establishment checklist, application form, account-registration guidance and application guidance are listed. A Department of Health directory entry retrieved in this research shows “Last Assured 10/01/2026” for the Medical Cannabis programme page. That supports, but does not prove, that the portal is accepting applications in Oct 2026. August 2026 secondary reporting (CannaCon, 24 Aug 2026): “cannabis business licensing in South Dakota is open to some applications. This includes Dispensary licenses, Manufacturing licenses, Cultivation licenses, and Testing licenses… The application process may take months to complete depending on inspections, local approval and state review.” The qualifier “to some applications” is the key point. Where a city/county cap is already filled (e.g. Sioux Falls’ 5, Spearfish’s 1, Yankton’s 2), a new state application for that locality cannot be awarded unless a slot opens (non-renewal, revocation, surrender, or a cap increase). Where no cap exists or the cap is not filled (e.g. Brookings on the excerpts retrieved), an application can proceed through local + state review. Therefore the correct answer to “are registrations open?” is: Yes, the Department of Health application route is published and, as of Aug–Oct 2026 secondary reporting, accepting applications, but availability is locality-by-locality. Verify live portal status and the specific locality’s cap/slot position before advising an applicant to file or pay a fee.

Scoring and lottery — the oversubscription mechanism. Statutory basis, SDCL 34-20G-72(2), opened directly at sdlegislature.gov/Statutes/34-20G-72, accessed 30 Oct 2026:

“Establishing a system to numerically score competing medical cannabis establishment applicants, in cases where more applicants apply than are allowed by the local government, that includes analysis of: (a) The preference of the local government; (b) In the case of dispensaries, the suitability of the proposed location and its accessibility for patients; (c) The character, veracity, background, qualifications, and relevant experience of principal officers and board members; and (d) The business plan proposed by the applicant, that in the case of a cultivation facility or dispensary shall include the ability to maintain an adequate supply of cannabis, plans to ensure safety and security of patrons and the community, procedures to be used to prevent diversion, and any plan for making cannabis available to low-income registered qualifying patients”

Rules implementation, via Puffy’s summary and ARSD chapter table: ARSD 44:90:03:15 — Department review of competitive applications, scoring criteria. ARSD 44:90:03:16 — Awarding, tie-breaking (random lottery drawing), notice to unsuccessful applicants, waitlist in lottery order. Documented example — Rapid City, 2022: 47 applicants, all tied on scoring, lottery drawing 9 Mar 2022 for 15 certificates. Puffy’s was first on the waitlist; litigation followed when a certificate holder failed to become operational and the Department’s handling of the waitlist / fee for the waitlist applicant was challenged. Puffy’s, LLC v. State, 2025 S.D. 10, filed 19 Feb 2025. Local lotteries are separate: Sioux Falls (17 Nov 2021, 5 licences) and Spearfish (Dec 2021, 1 licence) ran city lotteries under city ordinances. A city lottery win does not by itself confer a state certificate; the winner must still satisfy § 34-20G-55 and obtain the state certificate (and, in Sioux Falls, a certificate of occupancy).

4.5 Social equity provisions — none found

Finding: No social-equity programme was found in South Dakota’s medical-cannabis law in the sources retrieved. Specifically, no provision was found in SDCL chapter 34-20G or in the ARSD Article 44:90 excerpts retrieved for: set-asides or reserved licences for equity applicants; licensing priority, fee waivers/reductions, or scoring bonus for applicants from communities disproportionately affected by cannabis prohibition, or for persons with prior cannabis convictions (beyond the general background-check / disqualifying-felony rules, which run in the opposite direction); an equity fund, grant, loan or technical-assistance programme funded by cannabis fees or taxes; a definition of “social equity applicant” or “disproportionately impacted area.” What exists instead, and must not be mislabelled as equity: Low-income patient access as a scoring factor: SDCL 34-20G-72(2)(d) includes “any plan for making cannabis available to low-income registered qualifying patients” as part of the business-plan analysis for cultivation/dispensary applicants in a competitive scoring. That is a patient-access factor, not an applicant-equity preference. Patient fee sliding scale: SDCL 34-20G-72(11)(a) requires patient application/renewal fees on a sliding scale by household income ($75 standard / $20 low-income in practice). That is a patient fee policy, not an establishment equity policy. No state income-tax or fee relief for equity applicants was found; the absence of a state income tax benefits all operators equally and is not an equity programme. Flag: “No social equity provisions found” is a search result, not a proof of a negative across every rule, resolution and local ordinance. A locality could, in theory, adopt a local preference that functions as equity, but no such local equity ordinance was retrieved for any Part 3 jurisdiction. Verify against the full ARSD and any 2026 rules package before the final dossier states this as absolute.

4.6 Estimated startup costs — NON-OFFICIAL estimates only, and they disagree

No official South Dakota startup-cost estimate was found. The Department of Health does not publish a startup-cost guide in the sources retrieved. Every figure below is non-official, secondary, and in several cases outdated on fees. Do not present any of them as a Department of Health figure.

Non-official sourceDispensary startup range statedWhy it must be handled with care
Cannabis Training University, “How Much Does It Cost to Open a Dispensary? State-by-State” (2026 guide, search excerpt)South Dakota: $250,000–$500,000; application fee $5,000; licence fee $5,000 annually (as stated in that guide)Fee figures are outdated — state fee is now $9,000, not $5,000, and local fees in Sioux Falls alone exceed the guide’s licence figure. Range is a national-template estimate, not a South Dakota build-out quote.
SouthDakotaStateCannabis.org, “How to Open a Dispensary in South Dakota” (search excerpt)$400,000 to over $1,000,000; licence application fee $2,500 (as stated there); local certification $5,000–$50,000 by municipality; lease $40,000–$100,000/year; salaries ~$300,000/year; security/software/hardware ~$60,000/year; marketing $50,000; inventory at ~$1,500/lb average (as stated)$2,500 application fee conflicts with the official $9,000 state fee and with every other fee source. The $1,500/lb inventory figure is undated and unverified. Use only as an illustration of cost categories, not as a budget.
Quantum9, South Dakota consultants (Oct 2021 material, search excerpt)Dispensaries $1.2 million–$2 million in their experience2021, pre-$9,000 fee, pre-current local fees, and consultant marketing material, not an official estimate.
Cure8, “How To Open a Dispensary in South Dakota in 2024” (search excerpt)Annual operating cost $500,000–$2 million (general, not South Dakota-specific build-out)Also repeats the struck 15% excise as if current (see Part 2.7 warning) and states cultivation licences were “yet to receive” licences — outdated. Do not rely on this page.

What a realistic South Dakota budget must include, even though no total can be stated officially: (1) State fee: $9,000 per certificate per year (confirmed pattern). (2) Local fee(s): $1,500–$50,000+ depending on city and type (Sioux Falls dispensary initial $50,000 is the high end retrieved; most other cities $5,000 pattern). (3) Real estate / build-out / security / Metrc seed-to-sale compliance / testing ($1,000 per batch is reported by one operator in 2024 secondary reporting — operator statement, not a fee schedule, Verify). (4) Inventory, staffing, insurance, legal/compliance, and working capital until patient volume supports the site — all locality- and scale-dependent. (5) Cultivation and manufacturing startup costs: no South Dakota-specific credible estimate was retrieved at all. Unavailable — do not extrapolate the dispensary range to a grow or manufacturing facility.

Bottom line for Part 4.6: If the final dossier must give a number, it must be labelled “Non-official estimate, [source], [date], fees outdated — re-budget with current $9,000 state fee + current local fee schedule.” A single-point startup figure for South Dakota cannot be defended from the sources retrieved.

There are no adult-use licence types in force in South Dakota. The following are listed only so they are not mistaken for current law.

PART 5 — LICENCE DATA SOURCES

Do NOT try to list every licence holder in this Part — this is an inventory of sources. Method and headline finding: South Dakota does not publish a single downloadable CSV/XLSX master register of medical cannabis establishments, and does not publish medical cannabis sales. What it does publish is: (1) a DOH web list / data page for establishments and monthly programme data (web page + monthly PDFs — no bulk download found); (2) annual legislative reports (PDF, via the Legislature’s document system); (3) individual establishment inspection reports as separate PDFs on doh.sd.gov; (4) a DANR hemp programme web page with application forms (PDF) but no public hemp licence-holder list found; (5) a Secretary of State business-entity search (interactive search only, no free bulk list); (6) USDA hemp plan / search tools (interactive, login-gated for law enforcement; public search tool exists but no SD-specific extract was retrieved); (7) county/city licence records published only piecemeal (Minnehaha County ordinance + minutes; Pennington County zoning/licence ordinance; Sioux Falls and Rapid City municipal codes). No exact direct download link + file name could be verified for a DOH establishments CSV/XLSX, a DANR hemp licence list, or a tribal licence register, because no such downloadable file was found. That absence is itself a finding and is flagged below for each source — it is not filled in by guessing a URL. All sources accessed 30 Oct 2026 unless a different publication date is stated.

How to read sourcing flags in this Part: Opened directly = the page/PDF text was returned by browser.open on 30 Oct 2026. Search-result only / Verify = the fact comes only from a search-result snippet. It has NOT been confirmed by opening the official page directly, and must be re-checked live before being relied on. Secondary, dated = news, trade press or a research aggregator, usable only for non-law facts. Unavailable = no such source was found in the searches run for this report.

Parent programme pages — Verify live URLs before citing in the final HTML: medcannabis.sd.gov — cited as the programme portal on every DOH document opened directly (see §§5.1.2–5.1.3). doh.sd.gov/programs/medical-cannabis-program — cited in search results as the DOH programme page. Search-result only / Verify: this URL was not opened directly in this run.

5.1.4 DOH Establishment Inspection Reports (enforcement-adjacent source)

FieldFinding
Holder typeIndividual medical cannabis establishments, by name, type, city/ZIP, inspection date.
FormatIndividual PDFs on doh.sd.gov/media/, one per inspection. No consolidated enforcement-action list, spreadsheet or searchable inspection database was found.
How to accessVia DOH media library / programme pages; individual PDFs are indexed by search. Examples verified from search results (not opened individually in this run — Verify each before citing as an enforcement action): Northern Hills Alternative Health, Dispensary, Sturgis 57785, inspected 11/17/2025: 20251117_northernhills_d_sturgis.pdf; 1825 King of ReLeaf, Cultivation, Chamberlain 57325, 10/29/2025: 20251029_1825kingofreleaf_c_chamberlain.pdf; 1889 Farms, Cultivation, Rapid City 57701, 7/15/2026: 20260715_1889farms_c_rapidcity.pdf; Black Hills Cannabis Care, Cultivation, Rapid City 57702, 11/17/2025: 20251117_blackhillscannabiscare_c_rapidcity.pdf; Freedom Farms, Cultivation, Mission Hill 57046, 10/29/2025: 20251029_freedomfarms_c_missionhill.pdf; CC Health, Dispensary, Rapid City 57703, 7/16/2026: 20260716_cchealth_rapidcity.pdf; Black Hills Weed Shop, Dispensary, Rapid City 57702, 06/13/2025: 20250613_blackhillsweedshop_d_rapidcity.pdf. All accessed via search 30 Oct 2026.
Fields availableEstablishment name, type, city/ZIP, inspection date, and IN / OUT / COS / N/O / N/A findings by ARSD 44:90 section (operating procedures, security, training, labelling, inventory tracking via METRC, transport manifests, cultivation/manufacturing standards).
LimitationsAn inspection report showing “OUT” or “COS” findings is NOT by itself a suspension, revocation or fine. A separate consolidated list of suspensions, revocations, probationary statuses or fines was not found — Unavailable in this run. Note the 2025 Oversight Committee context: rule changes effective 15 July 2025 implemented fines for violations (see §5.1.6). Fines/probationary actions under those rules need a separate DOH source — Verify.
Seed-to-sale system named in these reportsMETRC — confirmed in the SFY 2024 report (contract Oct 2021, live Aug 2022) and in inspection-report text (“Confirm (through METRC) that all transfers have followed…”).
